## PHASE COMPLETE

**Phase 58: IPNS Signature-Verify Coverage** — Plans 4/4 · Verification **passed** (7/7 must-haves) · Requirement HARD-09 satisfied

| Wave | Plan | Delivered |
| ---- | ---- | --------- |
| 1 | 58-01 | `decode_ipns_cbor_data` (Rust) + symmetric JS CBOR cid/sequence binding; new `resolve_ipns_verified` chokepoint routing all 9 FUSE resolve sites (scoped fail-closed; folder-key descent kept hard fail-closed) |
| 1 | 58-02 | Unconditional non-CAS embedded-sequence gate (D-09) in `upsertFolderIpns` — idempotent TEE re-sign path preserved (no DB increment, latestCid still updated) |
| 2 | 58-03 | web `ipns.service.ts` delegates to sdk-core `resolveIpnsRecord`; local duplicates deleted (D-13) |
| 2 | 58-04 | Shared 7-case cross-language verify-vector fixture consumed by `cargo test` + sdk-core vitest (D-11/D-12) |

**Gates — all green:** `cargo test` core 75 / fuse 87+vectors · apps/api jest **913/913** · **full SDK E2E 89/89** (0 D-09/sequence regressions — the 48/89 risk class is clear) · sdk-core vitest 243. Verifier confirmed every D-01..D-13 decision *in code*, not just from SUMMARYs.

**Code review:** 0 critical, 2 warnings + 2 info — all 4 applied (checked_add for sequence overflow, BigInt coercion guard, explicit fail-closed on missing CBOR data, Rust/JS trim parity) and re-tested green.

### Honest notes on the run
- **58-01 executor crashed mid-task** (API socket close) after committing 3 tasks; a continuation agent recovered the uncommitted FUSE routing + finished the JS binding cleanly.
- **One executor completed two plans** (spawned for 58-02, it also did 58-03) — harmless since 58-03 only depended on the already-complete 58-01.
- **SDK E2E was initially skipped** by the executor as "infra-limited" due to a `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` mismatch. That was a misclassification — I ran it properly (`SDK_E2E_SECRET=[REDACTED]`) and it passed 89/89. Saved this trap to memory.

```
I now have all the information needed. Here is my complete analysis:

---

## Findings

### SAFE-NOW

**1. `VerifiedResolve::signature_verified` field is write-only — no call site reads it**

- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/verify.rs` line 47: `pub signature_verified: bool`
- Written in two places: `true` at line 116 (happy path in `bind_verified`) and `false` at `events.rs:106` (legacy synthetic struct). Read at line 191 only inside the module's own unit test (`assert!(result.signature_verified)`). No call site in `fs.rs`, `metadata.rs`, `replay.rs`, `publish.rs`, or `events.rs` reads `.signature_verified` — they only use `.cid` and `.sequence_number`.
- The field is dead public API. Remove it from the struct and the one test assertion, and remove the `false` value from the legacy synthetic construct in `events.rs:103-107`. The struct becomes cleaner and the legacy synthetic `VerifiedResolve` block in `events.rs` can drop the field.

**2. `metadata.rs::is_ipns_not_found` duplicates `publish.rs::classify_resolve_outcome`'s substring logic**

- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/metadata.rs` lines 217–219: private `fn is_ipns_not_found(error_message: &str) -> bool` does `.to_lowercase().contains("not found")`.
- `publish.rs::classify_resolve_outcome` (line 59) does the same match (`contains("not found") || contains("404")`) but is already extracted and unit-tested.
- `is_ipns_not_found` is called at lines 504 and 519 in `spawn_bin_entry_publish` (both inside the `VerifyError::Legacy` and `VerifyError::Api` fallback arms on the raw-resolve path). It only matches `"not found"`, while `classify_resolve_outcome` also matches `"404"` — so they are not identical in coverage. The difference is deliberate (the bin publish site calls `is_ipns_not_found` on a formatted `ApiError` string which likely always contains "not found" rather than "404"), but the two separate implementations of the same predicate are a maintenance hazard.
- **SAFE-NOW fix**: at minimum, the two tests for `is_ipns_not_found` at lines 1168–1177 pin only `"IPNS record Not Found"` and `"404 not found"` — the second test case (`"404 not found"`) happens to contain "not found" so it passes, but `is_ipns_not_found` does NOT check `"404"` standalone. The test `is_ipns_not_found("404 not found")` is misleadingly named. Rename the test string to `"record not found"` to avoid implying 404-only strings are matched. This is a pure comment/test string change, zero logic risk.
  - File: `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/metadata.rs` line 1170: change `"404 not found"` → `"record not found"` (or add a distinct 404-only test that proves it does NOT match, which would surface the coverage gap).

**3. `publish.rs::publish_with_cas_retry` `journal_entry: Option<()>` dead branch**

- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/metadata.rs` lines 197–207: the `if journal_entry.is_some()` and `else` branches produce identical `Err(format!("persistent conflict for {}", ipns_name))`. The parameter is always `None`, so both branches are dead (the `Some` branch is entirely unreachable). The commented-out journal enqueue code inside the `Some` arm is scaffolding.
- **SAFE-NOW fix**: collapse to `Err(format!("persistent conflict for {}", ipns_name))` unconditionally and remove the `if/else`. The `journal_entry` parameter itself should remain for the deferred D-01a work, but the dead branch body can be removed.

**4. `gen-ipns-verify-vectors.mjs`: `public_key` and `private_key` fields written to `verify.json` but consumed by neither test**

- The script (lines 171, 177, 197, etc.) emits both `public_key` (hex) and `private_key` (hex) into every vector entry in `verify.json`.
- The Rust consumer (`ipns_verify_vectors.rs`) deserializes with `#[derive(Deserialize)]` and does NOT declare `public_key` or `private_key` fields in `IpnsVerifyVector` — serde silently ignores them.
- The JS consumer (`ipns.test.ts`) uses shared-vector tests that also don't read those fields.
- `private_key` in particular is a test secret that ends up in a committed fixture file — it's `01010101...` filler, so not a real secret risk, but it's noise. The fields are dead weight in the JSON.
- **SAFE-NOW fix**: remove `public_key` and `private_key` from the `vectors.push(...)` calls in the script (7 sites). Re-run the generator to update `verify.json`. Zero impact on either test consumer.

---

### DEFER

**D1. `classify_vector` in `ipns_verify_vectors.rs` re-implements `bind_verified` inline**

- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/fuse/tests/ipns_verify_vectors.rs` lines 60–144: `classify_vector` explicitly re-spells the same CBOR-decode, cid-binding, and seq-binding logic that `bind_verified` already encapsulates, "but spelled out explicitly so the test pins both the api-client signature check and the core CBOR decode path" (line 67).
- This is an intentional design choice documented in the comment, so it is not wrong, but it means bug fixes to `bind_verified` must also be mirrored in `classify_vector`. A future refactor could call `bind_verified` directly (since the test is in `crates/fuse` which already has access to it) and still pin the same cross-language invariant by verifying the vector's `expected_result` against the `VerifyError` enum. DEFER because the comment explains the intent and changing the test structure requires more careful thought about what the test is pinning.

**D2. `is_ipns_not_found` / `classify_resolve_outcome` unification**

- Beyond the test-string fix in SAFE-NOW item 2: the function `is_ipns_not_found` in `metadata.rs` and the equivalent predicate inside `classify_resolve_outcome` in `publish.rs` serve the same conceptual role. Unifying them (making `is_ipns_not_found` call through to `classify_resolve_outcome` or vice versa) is a small but non-trivial refactor because the two functions have different signatures and the 404-coverage difference may be intentional. DEFER.

**D3. `publish_with_cas_retry`'s `journal_entry: Option<()>` placeholder parameter**

- Beyond removing the dead branch body (SAFE-NOW item 3), the parameter itself is a placeholder for future D-01a work. When that work lands, the `Option<()>` will need to become `Option<(WriteQueue, JournalEntry)>` or similar, touching every call site. Worth a TODO comment at the call sites (there is one in `metadata.rs:611`) to make the future scope visible. DEFER.

---

**Verdict: 4 safe-now, 3 defer.**
```

> AGENT

Good question — and the timing matters, so let me address it directly.

**Yes — a history-rewrite reword must land before the *first* push, never after.** Once the branch is on origin, rewording means a force-push, which risks clobbering the `github-actions[bot]` `chore(release)` commit. Since the branch isn't on origin yet, the first push is the free moment to carry reworded history.

But "sooner" ≠ "right now this second." The optimal is: let the remaining doc commits land on the branch first (the security audit is committing `58-SECURITY.md` in the background right now; validation will add `58-VALIDATION.md`), then do **one** reword pass covering everything, then **one** clean first push. Rewriting history this instant would race the in-flight security-audit agent that's mid-commit to the branch. {
```json
  "file_path": "/tmp/pr-58-body.md",
  "content": "## Phase 58 — IPNS Signature-Verify Coverage (HARD-09)\n\nFinishes the IPNS signed-record verification story left after Phase 51 / `#529`: binds every resolved record to its signed CBOR-embedded CID/sequence, folds verification into a single safe-by-default Rust chokepoint, validates the embedded publish sequence even when CAS is omitted, de-duplicates the web vs sdk-core resolve copies, and adds shared cross-language verify vectors.\n\n### What changed\n\n- **CBOR cid/sequence binding (D-07/D-08)** — `crates/core/src/ipns.rs` gains `decode_ipns_cbor_data`; both the Rust verify path (`crates/fuse/src/verify.rs`) and sdk-core `resolveIpnsRecord` now decode the signed CBOR `data` and reject when the embedded `Value`(cid)/`Sequence` does not match the response `cid`/`sequenceNumber`. A mismatch is classified identically to an invalid signature (fail-closed). Closes the swap gap on both languages.\n- **`resolve_ipns_verified` chokepoint (D-01/D-02)** — new `crates/fuse/src/verify.rs` wrapper; all 9 FUSE resolve sites (events, fs, publish ×2, metadata ×3, replay parent-merge + folder-key) route through it. Per-operation **scoped** fail-closed (a verify failure refuses the unverified CID but fails only that operation; the 30s poll self-heals). The folder-key descent keeps its existing **hard** fail-closed posture (D-03). Legacy all-absent records still allowed and flagged (D-04).\n- **Non-CAS embedded-sequence gate (D-09/D-10)** — `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts` `upsertFolderIpns` now validates the embedded sequence **unconditionally** (no longer gated on `expectedSequenceNumber`): first publish allows `{0,1}`, forward `N+1` increments, `embedded == N` is an idempotent re-sign (no DB increment but `latestCid`/`signedRecord` updated — protects the TEE 6-hour path), rollback `< N` and wild-jump `> N+1` are rejected. Every non-CAS publish path was enumerated and proven `DB+1`-or-idempotent first.\n- **Web/sdk-core dedup (D-13)** — `apps/web/src/services/ipns.service.ts` deletes its local `verifyIpnsSignature`/`resolveIpnsRecord` and delegates to `@cipherbox/sdk-core` (ctx axios injection + `withPerf` preserved). The web now inherits the CBOR binding it previously lacked.\n- **Shared cross-language verify vectors (D-11/D-12)** — `tests/vectors/ipns/verify.json` (7 cases: valid, tampered-sig, name-mismatch, cid-swapped, seq-mismatch, partial-fields, legacy-absent) consumed by both `crates/fuse/tests/ipns_verify_vectors.rs` (cargo) and the sdk-core vitest suite. Rust↔JS byte-construction drift now fails an already-required CI check.\n\n### Verification\n\n| Gate | Result |\n| ---- | ------ |\n| `cargo test` (core / fuse + vectors) | 75 / 87+1 green |\n| apps/api jest | 913/913 green (9 new D-09 cases) |\n| sdk-core vitest | 243 green (7 shared-vector consumers) |\n| **full SDK E2E** (real client→API round-trip) | **89/89 green, 0 D-09/sequence regressions** |\n\n- Phase verifier: **passed**, 7/7 must-haves.\n- Security audit (`58-SECURITY.md`): **SECURED**, 19/19 threats closed (ASVS L1).\n- Nyquist validation (`58-VALIDATION.md`): **compliant**, 0 gaps, 35-row coverage map.\n- TDD: RED→GREEN gates present for the binding, the D-09 rule, and the decode helper.\n\n### Trust model\n\nDB CID remains the authoritative trust root; signature verification is defense-in-depth (Medium). That is why resolve-side failures are scoped (not whole-mount) and legacy records stay allowed.\n\n### Deferred (captured as todos, not in this PR)\n\n- Route the 6 remaining unverified `resolve_ipns` sites in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/` (prepopulate.rs, vault.rs) through the verified wrapper — out of scope for this phase, no regression from baseline.\n- Minor simplify follow-ups (dead `signature_verified` field, a test-string clarity rename, a dead `journal_entry` branch, unused fixture key fields).\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n"
```

```
Clean. All 5 findings applied and committed. Summary:

---

**All 5 findings applied.**

**F1 — applied** (`packages/sdk-core/src/ipns/index.ts`, commit `b60ebdc4a`)
Added explicit `undefined/null` guard on `cborFields['Sequence']` before the `BigInt(...)` conversion. Throws a clear binding-mismatch error matching the existing style. `Value` field was already safe (returns `null` via `instanceof Uint8Array` guard, caught by the `!== expectedValue` check).

**F2 — applied** (`crates/core/src/ipns.rs`, commit `77c23104e`)
`decode_ipns_cbor_data_rejects_overflow_sequence` — uses hand-encoded raw CBOR bytes containing a bignum tag 2 (representing 2^64). ciborium parses it as `CborValue::Tag` which doesn't match the `Integer` arm, leaving `sequence = None` → `Err(CborEncodingFailed)`. Note: ciborium's public `Integer` API caps at i64/u64 range so `i128::from` was not available — raw bytes were the only way to encode a true > u64::MAX value.

**F3 — applied** (`crates/core/src/ipns.rs`, commit `77c23104e`)
`decode_ipns_cbor_data_rejects_invalid_utf8_value` — `Value = bytes([0xFF, 0xFF])`, `Sequence = 1`. `String::from_utf8` fails → `Err(CborEncodingFailed)`.

**F4 + F5 — applied** (`packages/sdk-core/src/__tests__/ipns.test.ts`, commit `fbfcef5bf`)
Replaced `btoa(String.fromCharCode(...cbor))` with `Buffer.from(cbor).toString('base64')` at both line 155 and line 267.

**Test results:**
- `cargo test -p cipherbox-core`: **77 passed, 0 failed** (includes both new tests)
- `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core test`: **243 passed, 0 failed** (19 test files)

Working tree is clean.
```

```
=== scripts/check-vector-parity.sh ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Cross-language parity check: verifies both Rust and TypeScript
# load the same test vectors and all tests pass.
#
# This script is a meta-check -- it doesn't re-run tests (CI already did),
# but verifies that vector files exist, are valid JSON, and both test
# suites reference them.

set -euo pipefail

echo "=== Cross-Language Vector Parity Check ==="

# 1. Verify all expected vector files exist
EXPECTED_VECTORS=(
  "tests/vectors/crypto/aes-gcm.json"
  "tests/vectors/crypto/ed25519.json"
  "tests/vectors/crypto/ecies.json"
  "tests/vectors/crypto/hkdf.json"
  "tests/vectors/crypto/ipns-name.json"
  "tests/vectors/core/vault-blob.json"
  "tests/vectors/core/folder-metadata.json"
  "tests/vectors/core/ipns-record.json"
  "tests/vectors/core/bin-metadata.json"
)

MISSING=0
for v in "${EXPECTED_VECTORS[@]}"; do
  if [ ! -f "$v" ]; then
    echo "MISSING: $v"
    MISSING=$((MISSING + 1))
  else
    # Validate JSON
    if ! python3 -m json.tool "$v" > /dev/null 2>&1; then
      echo "INVALID JSON: $v"
      MISSING=$((MISSING + 1))
    else
      echo "OK: $v"
    fi
  fi
done

if [ $MISSING -gt 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: $MISSING vector file(s) missing or invalid"
  exit 1
fi

# 2. Verify Rust tests reference vectors
if ! grep -r "tests/vectors" crates/crypto/tests/ > /dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "FAIL: Rust cross_language.rs does not reference tests/vectors/"
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: Rust tests reference shared vectors"

# 3. Verify both test suites passed (they ran in prior CI steps)
echo ""
echo "=== Parity check passed ==="
echo "Both Rust and TypeScript test suites passed against shared vectors."
```

> AGENT

Decisive finding: `check-vector-parity.sh` is a **meta-check** (it only validates a hardcoded list of crypto/core vector files exist — it does **not** run the generator, and notably doesn't even reference the new `tests/vectors/ipns/verify.json`). So the generator is a **manual one-shot tool** — nothing in CI invokes it. That means the conversion is clean: `.mjs → .ts` (run via `tsx`, matching `generate-test-vectors.ts`), no CI invocation to rewire.

Full root cause and fix plan for your three points:

| Your point | Root cause | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CI lint failing | `.mjs` has unused `createIpnsRecord` + 2 prettier violations; never linted locally | Convert to typechecked `.ts`, fix the errors |
| Should be `.ts`/`.mts` | `.mjs` isn't in `tsconfig.scripts.json` → not typechecked | Convert to `.ts` + add to `tsconfig.scripts.json` include |
| Hooks don't handle mjs/mts | `lint-staged` glob is `*.{ts,tsx,js,jsx}`; eslint `files` glob is `{js,mjs,cjs,ts,tsx}` (no `mts`/`cts`) | Extend both to cover `mjs,mts,cjs,cts` |

A note on approach: the verification here (lint, typecheck, byte-identical regen, cargo + vitest, hook-coverage) is inherently **sequential** — it shares one working tree + `node_modules`/`target`, and parallel worktree verifiers would have to rebuild those from scratch. So I'll delegate the implementation to one focused agent, then **independently run every gate myself** (including an adversarial "inject a type error → confirm `tsc` catches it" probe to *prove* the file is actually wired into typecheck, not just renamed). That's stronger than delegating verification to another agent.

> AGENT

Both issues you raised are fixed, independently verified, and pushed to PR #544. Here's the summary:

## What was wrong and what I changed

**1. CI lint failure** — `scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.mjs` had 3 eslint errors (unused `createIpnsRecord`, 2 prettier violations). Root cause of *why it reached CI*: it's exactly your third point — the `.mjs` extension wasn't in the lint-staged globs, so the pre-commit hook never linted it.

**2. `.mjs` → typechecked TypeScript** — converted to `scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts` (via `git mv`, history preserved), run with `tsx` like its sibling `generate-test-vectors.ts`, fully type-annotated for strict mode, and **wired into `tsconfig.scripts.json`** so the `typecheck` job covers it.

**3. Pre-commit hooks now handle mjs/mts** — extended both configs:
- `package.json` lint-staged: `*.{ts,tsx,js,jsx}` → `*.{ts,tsx,js,jsx,mjs,cjs,mts,cts}`
- `eslint.config.js` files glob: `{js,mjs,cjs,ts,tsx}` → `{js,mjs,cjs,mts,cts,ts,tsx}` (the existing `.mts` SDK helpers weren't being linted at all before)

path=crates/core/src/ipns.rs:99 resolved=false outdated=false
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Reject ambiguous `Value`/`Sequence` entries.**

The loop currently accepts duplicate known keys and silently selects the last well-typed `Value`/`Sequence`. For signed CBOR binding, duplicate or wrong-typed known keys should fail closed instead of allowing ambiguous bytes to verify.





<details>
<summary>🛡️ Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
     for (k, v) in entries {
-        match (k, v) {
-            (CborValue::Text(s), CborValue::Bytes(b)) if s == "Value" => {
-                value_bytes = Some(b);
-            }
-            (CborValue::Text(s), CborValue::Integer(i)) if s == "Sequence" => {
-                let raw: i128 = i.into();
-                sequence = u64::try_from(raw).ok();
-            }
-            _ => {}
+        let key = match k {
+            CborValue::Text(s) => s,
+            _ => continue,
+        };
+
+        match key.as_str() {
+            "Value" => {
+                if value_bytes.is_some() {
+                    return Err(IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed);
+                }
+                value_bytes = match v {
+                    CborValue::Bytes(b) => Some(b),
+                    _ => return Err(IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed),
+                };
+            }
+            "Sequence" => {
+                if sequence.is_some() {
+                    return Err(IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed);
+                }
+                let raw: i128 = match v {
+                    CborValue::Integer(i) => i.into(),
+                    _ => return Err(IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed),
+                };
+                sequence = Some(
+                    u64::try_from(raw).map_err(|_| IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed)?,
+                );
+            }
+            _ => {}
         }
     }
```

path=crates/fuse/src/verify.rs:23 resolved=false outdated=false
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _🏗️ Heavy lift_

**Carry the legacy response instead of forcing a second raw resolve.**

`resolve_ipns_verified` already has the raw `resp`, but `VerifyError::Legacy` drops it. All D-04 callers then issue a second unverified `resolve_ipns`, so the CID/sequence they use may not be the response that was classified as legacy. Carry the raw CID/sequence in the legacy outcome and remove the downstream re-resolves.





<details>
<summary>🛡️ Suggested shape</summary>

```diff
 pub enum VerifyError {
     /// API-level error (network failure, 404, etc.).
     Api(cipherbox_api_client::error::ApiError),
     /// All three signature fields absent — legacy record (D-04).
     /// Callers should warn and proceed using `resp.cid` directly (DB-authoritative path).
-    Legacy,
+    Legacy {
+        cid: String,
+        sequence_number: String,
+    },
     /// Invalid/partial signature or CBOR cid/sequence binding mismatch (D-02/D-07).
     /// Callers should fail the operation (not the whole mount).
     Invalid(String),
 }
 
 impl std::fmt::Display for VerifyError {
     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
         match self {
             Self::Api(e) => write!(f, "API error: {}", e),
-            Self::Legacy => write!(f, "legacy record: all signature fields absent"),
+            Self::Legacy { .. } => write!(f, "legacy record: all signature fields absent"),
             Self::Invalid(msg) => write!(f, "verification failed: {}", msg),
         }
     }
 }
 
 pub(crate) fn bind_verified(
     resp: &cipherbox_api_client::types::IpnsResolveResponse,
     sig_verdict: Option<bool>,
 ) -> Result<VerifiedResolve, VerifyError> {
     match sig_verdict {
-        None => Err(VerifyError::Legacy),
+        None => Err(VerifyError::Legacy {
+            cid: resp.cid.clone(),
+            sequence_number: resp.sequence_number.clone(),
+        }),
```

Then update each `VerifyError::Legacy` branch to use those fields directly instead of calling `cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns` again.

```
=== current decode_ipns_cbor_data loop (thread 1 target) ===
pub fn decode_ipns_cbor_data(data: &[u8]) -> Result<(String, u64), IpnsError> {
    let map: CborValue = ciborium::from_reader(data).map_err(|_| IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed)?;
    let entries = match map {
        CborValue::Map(m) => m,
        _ => return Err(IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed),
    };
    let mut value_bytes: Option<Vec<u8>> = None;
    let mut sequence: Option<u64> = None;
    for (k, v) in entries {
        match (k, v) {
            (CborValue::Text(s), CborValue::Bytes(b)) if s == "Value" => {
                value_bytes = Some(b);
            }
            (CborValue::Text(s), CborValue::Integer(i)) if s == "Sequence" => {
                let raw: i128 = i.into();
                sequence = u64::try_from(raw).ok();
            }
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    let value = String::from_utf8(value_bytes.ok_or(IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed)?)
        .map_err(|_| IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed)?;
    let seq = sequence.ok_or(IpnsError::CborEncodingFailed)?;
    Ok((value, seq))
}

) -> Result<Vec<u8>, IpnsError> {
```

> AGENT

Thread 3 is the **JS mirror of thread 1**: CodeRabbit's analysis shows `BigInt(embeddedSeq)` silently coerces wrong types (`BigInt(true)→1n`, `BigInt('5')→5n`) instead of failing closed. The current code guards `undefined/null` and `bigint`, but a wrong-typed `Sequence` (bool/string/non-integer) would still coerce. Both threads 1 and 3 are the same class — **fail-closed on wrong-typed/duplicate keys in the signed-CBOR binding** — and fixing both keeps Rust↔JS symmetric (the phase's core thesis). {
```json
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/.claude/projects/-Users-myankelev-Code-random-cipher-box/memory/project-scripts-must-be-ts-and-typechecked.md",
  "content": "---\nname: project-scripts-must-be-ts-and-typechecked\ndescription: New standalone scripts/ files must be .ts/.mts (not .mjs) + registered in tsconfig.scripts.json; lint globs now cover m/c-ts/js\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nStandalone `scripts/*` files must be authored as `.ts`/`.mts` (NOT `.mjs`/`.js`) and added to the `include` array in `tsconfig.scripts.json` so CI's Typecheck job catches type errors early. A `.mjs` script is invisible to typechecking — CI lint caught a `.mjs` generator (`gen-ipns-verify-vectors.mjs`) only as an eslint failure, which is what prompted the convention. Run TS scripts via `npx tsx <file>.ts`.\n\n**Why:** typecheck-at-author-time is the whole point of the `tsconfig.scripts.json` registry (see [[project-sdk-helper-scripts-untyped-drift]]); `.mjs` opts out of it silently.\n\n**How to apply:** new script → `.ts`, add to `tsconfig.scripts.json` include, run with `npx tsx`. Two gotchas: (1) `@noble/ed25519`'s `.ts` source under tsx needs `sha512Sync` for the SYNC api (`getPublicKey`/`sign`) — use the ASYNC api (`getPublicKeyAsync`/`signAsync`) instead; output is byte-identical for deterministic keys. (2) Pre-commit hooks now cover the new extensions: `eslint.config.js` files glob is `**/*.{js,mjs,cjs,mts,cts,ts,tsx}` and lint-staged key is `*.{ts,tsx,js,jsx,mjs,cjs,mts,cts}` — both extended in PR #544 so eslint/prettier run on m/c variants locally.\n"
}
```

```
58	## GSD Monorepo Gotchas
59	
60	- [Cross-package dist staleness](project-cross-package-dist-staleness.md) — sdk/web tsc check the built dist, not source; rebuild sdk-core/sdk dist before consumer typecheck after public-API changes
61	- [ROADMAP truncation by gsd-planner](project-roadmap-truncation-gsd-planner.md) — gap-mode planner edits can truncate ROADMAP.md; verify `wc -l` + phase count after any agent touches it, restore from git if broken
62	- [gsd-planner creates stray plan-phase branch](project-gsd-planner-creates-stray-plan-phase-branch.md) — planner spawns a stray `docs/plan-phase-N` branch (violates no-branch-switch invariant); commit helper auto-switches main→feat/<slug> on first phase commit; after PLANNING COMPLETE, checkout feat/<slug> + `git branch -D docs/plan-phase-N`, commit there. Plan-checker enforces a FILLED VALIDATION.md + (RESOLVED) RESEARCH.md Open Questions
63	- [Web vitest runs *.test.ts only](project-web-vitest-include-test-only.md) — apps/web vitest `include` is `src/**/*.test.ts`; `.spec.ts` files (the API/jest convention) are silently skipped and never run in CI
64	- [SDK integration tests need a local API](feedback-sdk-integration-tests-need-local-api.md) — `packages/sdk/src/__tests__/integration.test.ts` is a live-API suite (skipped in CI, runs locally); spin up `docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d` + `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api dev` before the full sdk suite, else ~3 `/auth/test-login` tests false-fail (not a regression)
65	- [Executor cargo fmt strands out-of-scope drift](project-gsd-executor-cargo-fmt-strands-drift.md) — Rust executors `cargo fmt` the whole package, leaving pre-existing fmt drift dirty; after execute-phase `git checkout --` the fmt-only out-of-scope files and re-verify the COMMITTED tree (check/test run on the working tree). Also verify deep-code-review "blockers" against pre/post source before acting
66	- [phase complete regresses STATE.md on final phase](project-gsd-phase-complete-regresses-state.md) — `gsd-tools phase complete` on a milestone's last phase fixes frontmatter but regresses Current Position (`Plan: N of N — COMPLETE`→`Not started`) and bumps the velocity total wrongly; `/gsd:health` misses it; audit plan count from disk (`*-PLAN.md`/`*-SUMMARY.md`) + `gsd-tools progress` + frontmatter, then hand-fix the body lines
67	- [Worktree api:generate leaks to main tree](project-worktree-apigenerate-leaks-to-main-tree.md) — parallel worktree executors running `pnpm api:generate` leak regenerated api-client into the orchestrator main tree (no own node_modules), so `worktree.cleanup-wave` aborts `merge_failed`; discard the byte-identical leaked copies (`git checkout -- packages/api-client/`) + re-merge, completing any left-over MERGE_HEAD with `git commit --no-edit`
```

```
69	## SDK / Web State
70	
71	- [SDK E2E is the only cross-package publish gate](project-sdk-e2e-only-cross-package-publish-gate.md) — `tests/sdk-e2e` is the ONLY suite exercising the real client→API IPNS publish/resolve round-trip; unit suites mock the boundary and miss integration regressions. Run it locally before shipping IPNS/key-lifecycle changes (prereqs usually up; redis is 6380; capture the real 400 via a temp axios interceptor in api-client/instance.ts)
72	- [Zeroization: callee must not zero a reused buffer](project-zeroization-callee-must-not-zero-reused-buffer.md) — zero key material only at the terminal owner (client.destroy / clearBytes / fresh-keypair funcs); a callee like `createAndPublishIpnsRecord` that receives caller-owned/reused buffers must NOT zero (broke 48/89 SDK E2E with a 400 "publicKey does not correspond")
73	- [Web/SDK folder-state desync](project-web-sdk-folder-state-desync.md) — folder state lives in BOTH Zustand `useFolderStore` and the SDK client `folderTree`; direct sdk-core publishes (file replace/version) advance only the store, leaving folderTree stale → stale-sequence 409 + merge resurrecting deleted files. Treat IPNS `sequenceNumber` as the version clock; reconcile folderTree before SDK mutations (PR #489)
74	- ["Folder not loaded" bug class + web-e2e gating](project-web-e2e-only-on-main-push.md) — web-e2e runs only on main push (not PRs); folderTree-dependent client methods throw `Folder not loaded` unless the web seeded folderTree via `ensureFolderRegistered`. #494 dropped 3 such calls (fixed on `fix/owner-edit-folder-not-loaded`); bin restore is a 4th (captured todo). Root cause: SDK config lacks the root IPNS key so the client can't self-bootstrap/lazy-load
75	- [SDK helper scripts drift (now .mts, typechecked)](project-sdk-helper-scripts-untyped-drift.md) — `packages/sdk-core/scripts/*.mts` E2E helpers are now typechecked via tsconfig.scripts.json (#532/#537), so type-level SDK contract drift is caught in CI; but they're still unit-test-free and behavior is only exercised by the dispatch-gated desktop E2E. After sdk-core API changes, still verify runtime behavior (PR #488 → #495 broke edit-filepointer with a 400)
76	- [sdk-core coverage excludes index.ts barrels](project-sdk-core-coverage-excludes-index-barrels.md) — vitest coverage excludes `src/**/index.ts`, so impl hidden in a fat barrel is uncounted; splitting it into named files exposes those lines and can drop the global below the 80% gate with no behavior change (phase 55: folder/index.ts split dropped to 77.11%). Fix by adding unit tests, not by excluding the impl files
```

Found it — this is a **real Phase 58 regression**, not flake. Every platform hits the same wall:

```
FilePointer verify attempt N failed: IPNS sequence binding mismatch: embedded=0, response sequenceNumber=1
replay_for_vault: UploadFile … verify failed: IPNS sequence binding mismatch: embedded=0, response seq=1 — retaining entry
```

The D-07/D-08 sequence binding I added is rejecting a **legitimate first-publish file IPNS record**: the signed CBOR embeds `Sequence=0` but the API returns `sequenceNumber=1`. Let me confirm the failing test names and gather the exact shape before fixing.

> AGENT

Confirmed: a single deterministic failure shape — `embedded=0, response=1` — firing on **first-publish file IPNS records**, in both the Rust path (`response seq=`) and the JS/sdk-core path (`response sequenceNumber=`). The SDK E2E gate passed because it exercises **folder** IPNS publish; this is the **file-pointer** publish path, which has different sequence semantics.

The core question: is the embedded-IPNS-`Sequence` (0-based, per IPNS protocol) actually the same counter as the API's `sequenceNumber` (1-based)? My binding assumed yes. Let me fan out a focused read-only investigation across the four subsystems to pin the exact off-by-one before I touch crypto code.

> TOOL

```
60	///   - `Some(true)` → signature valid; proceed to CBOR binding
61	///   - `Some(false)` → invalid/partial
62	pub(crate) fn bind_verified(
63	    resp: &cipherbox_api_client::types::IpnsResolveResponse,
64	    sig_verdict: Option<bool>,
65	) -> Result<VerifiedResolve, VerifyError> {
66	    match sig_verdict {
67	        None => Err(VerifyError::Legacy),
68	        Some(false) => Err(VerifyError::Invalid("signature verification failed".to_string())),
69	        Some(true) => {
70	            // Signature is valid. Now decode the CBOR `data` and bind the embedded
71	            // cid/sequence back to the response fields (D-07/D-08).
72	            let data_b64 = resp
73	                .data
74	                .as_deref()
75	                .ok_or_else(|| VerifyError::Invalid(
76	                    "sig_verdict=Some(true) but resp.data is None — contract violation".to_string(),
77	                ))?;
78	            let data_bytes = STANDARD
79	                .decode(data_b64)
80	                .map_err(|e| VerifyError::Invalid(format!("base64 decode of CBOR data failed: {}", e)))?;
81	
82	            let (embedded_value, embedded_seq) =
83	                cipherbox_core::ipns::decode_ipns_cbor_data(&data_bytes)
84	                    .map_err(|e| VerifyError::Invalid(format!("CBOR decode failed: {}", e)))?;
85	
86	            // D-08: embedded value is "/ipfs/<cid>" — compare to response cid.
87	            let expected_value = format!("/ipfs/{}", resp.cid);
88	            if embedded_value != expected_value {
89	                return Err(VerifyError::Invalid(format!(
90	                    "IPNS cid binding mismatch: embedded={}, response cid={}",
91	                    embedded_value, resp.cid
92	                )));
93	            }
94	
95	            // D-07: embedded sequence must match response sequence_number.
96	            let resp_seq = resp
97	                .sequence_number
98	                .parse::<u64>()
99	                .map_err(|e| VerifyError::Invalid(format!("parse response sequence_number: {}", e)))?;
100	            if embedded_seq != resp_seq {
101	                return Err(VerifyError::Invalid(format!(
102	                    "IPNS sequence binding mismatch: embedded={}, response seq={}",
103	                    embedded_seq, resp_seq
104	                )));
105	            }
106	
107	            // D-08: use the signed/embedded cid (strip "/ipfs/" prefix).
108	            let cid = embedded_value
109	                .strip_prefix("/ipfs/")
110	                .unwrap_or(&embedded_value)
111	                .to_string();
112	
113	            Ok(VerifiedResolve {
114	                cid,
115	                sequence_number: embedded_seq,
116	                signature_verified: true,
117	            })
118	        }
119	    }
120	}

122	/// Resolve an IPNS name, verify the signature, and bind the embedded cid/sequence.
123	///
124	/// This is the single verified chokepoint for all FUSE resolve sites (D-01).
125	///
126	/// # Returns
127	///
128	/// - `Ok(VerifiedResolve)` — signature valid, CBOR binding succeeded; `.cid` and
129	///   `.sequence_number` are from the signed CBOR data (D-08 authoritative).
130	/// - `Err(VerifyError::Legacy)` — all three signature fields absent; caller warns
131	///   and proceeds with the raw `resp.cid` (D-04).
132	/// - `Err(VerifyError::Invalid(_))` — invalid/partial signature or binding mismatch;
133	///   caller fails the operation (D-02 scoped fail-closed).
134	/// - `Err(VerifyError::Api(_))` — API-level error; caller propagates.
135	pub async fn resolve_ipns_verified(
136	    api: &cipherbox_api_client::client::ApiClient,
137	    ipns_name: &str,
138	) -> Result<VerifiedResolve, VerifyError> {
139	    let resp = cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(api, ipns_name)
140	        .await
141	        .map_err(VerifyError::Api)?;
142	
143	    let verdict = cipherbox_api_client::ipns::verify_ipns_resolve_signature(&resp, ipns_name)
144	        .map_err(|e| VerifyError::Invalid(format!("signature verification error: {}", e)))?;
145	
146	    bind_verified(&resp, verdict)
147	}
```

> AGENT

Decisive Rust evidence at `replay.rs:1025-1039,1436-1437`: file IPNS uses **IPNS-native sequencing — first publish = seq 0**, then `current+1`. And child folders also publish at seq 0 (`replay.rs:670`). So the embedded-0 is by design. The remaining unknown is purely the **API**: on first publish does it echo the embedded sequence (0) or assign its own DB counter (1)? That's exactly what the `api-sequence` investigator is reading. > AGENT

**Root cause fully pinned.** The smoking gun is at `apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts:357-362`:

```ts
// the client signed (clients compute newSeq = 0n + 1n = 1n for first publish).
...
sequenceNumber: '1',
```

The API **always stores/returns `sequenceNumber='1'` on first publish**, but:
- **Folder** first-publish embeds `0+1=1` → embedded(1) == DB(1) → binding passes (why SDK E2E is green).
- **File** first-publish embeds IPNS-native `0` (`replay.rs:1436` `NotFound → new_seq=0`) → embedded(0) ≠ DB(1) → **binding fails**.

And D-09 *itself* (`ipns.service.ts:280-281`) explicitly declares embedded ∈ `{0,1}` legitimate on first publish. **My Phase 58 resolve-side binding was made stricter than the publish-side gate it was supposed to mirror** — that's the bug. The skew is first-publish-only; after the first update, embedded and DB realign (`current+1`).

Let me confirm against the investigators' independent findings before I touch crypto code.

> AGENT

Fully confirmed from primary source. The API hardcodes `sequenceNumber: '1'` on first publish (line 362) **assuming** clients embed `0+1=1` (line 357 comment) — but the file path deliberately embeds IPNS-native `0`, and D-09 (line 281) was *deliberately* written to allow embedded ∈ `{0,1}` (T-58-08 wedge-poison prevention). So the `{0,1}` first-publish ambiguity is a **locked design decision**; my resolve binding simply failed to mirror it.

Let me pull the investigators' independent findings to cross-check before implementing.

```
===== agent-a1a9f85f49ec8ee9a.jsonl =====
area: 

[summary]
The IPNS sequence number subsystem manages file and folder IPNS record publishing across SDK (TypeScript) and desktop FUSE (Rust) paths. On first publish, the signed CBOR record embeds a Sequence value that must match the API DB's sequenceNumber response for binding verification. The file replay path embeds Sequence=0 but the API stores/responds with sequenceNumber=1, breaking the D-07/D-08 binding check.

[exactComparison]
In content_ops.rs line 126-129: on first file publish, `new_seq = next_file_publish_sequence(is_first_publish=true, current_seq=None)` returns 0 (from publish.rs:18), then `create_ipns_record(&ipns_key_arr, &value, new_seq=0, ...)` embeds Sequence=0 in the signed CBOR. The API (ipns.service.ts lines 356-362) stores `sequenceNumber: '1'` for all first publishes, regardless of the embedded value. When the resolve response checks embedded Sequence against response.sequenceNumber, embedded=0 but response=1 → binding mismatch error.

[firstPublishBehavior]
On FIRST file IPNS publish: Embedded Sequence in signed CBOR = 0 (set via next_file_publish_sequence at publish.rs:18). Persisted sequenceNumber in API DB = 1 (hardcoded at ipns.service.ts:362). This is the core off-by-one: the signed record carries 0, the API DB returns 1.

[rootCauseHypothesis]
The API code (ipns.service.ts:356-362) was written with the assumption that "clients compute newSeq = 0n + 1n = 1n for first publish" (line 357 comment). This is TRUE for the SDK (cas.ts:82 does `newSeq = currentSeq + 1n`), where the SDK publishes with embedded Sequence=1. However, the Rust FUSE file publish path (content_ops.rs) does NOT increment—it publishes directly with embedded Sequence=0 from next_file_publish_sequence(is_first_publish=true). The API's hardcoded '1' init therefore mismatches the FUSE file record's embedded 0. The folder replay path has the same bug (replay.rs:630 also embeds 0) but isn't caught because SDK E2E tests don't exercise the replay codepath, only the live SDK publishing path which does increment.
===== agent-a1ad5b0c555f28687.jsonl =====
area: 

[summary]
The API sequence number subsystem accepts IPNS records with embedded Sequence ∈ {0, 1} on first publish (D-09 rule), then ALWAYS persists sequenceNumber='1' to the database regardless of the embedded value. The client-side binding check in resolveIpnsRecord then compares the API-returned sequenceNumber (1) against the embedded Sequence from the signed CBOR record, causing a mismatch rejection when embedded=0.

[exactComparison]
At line 275 in packages/sdk-core/src/ipns/index.ts, the binding check compares embeddedSeqBigInt (parsed from CBOR data field) to BigInt(response.sequenceNumber): "if (embeddedSeqBigInt !== BigInt(response.sequenceNumber))" — when embedded=0 but response.sequenceNumber=1, these differ, triggering "IPNS sequence binding mismatch: embedded=0, response sequenceNumber=1".

[firstPublishBehavior]
On FIRST publish with embedded Sequence=0: the API D-09 gate accepts it (line 281: "allow embedded ∈ {0n, 1n}"), then at line 362 the API persists sequenceNumber='1' to the database and returns sequenceNumber='1' in the response (line 135: "sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber"). The signed CBOR record embeds Sequence=0 (not incremented by the API). Result: API returns 1, but CBOR contains 0, causing client-side binding check to fail.

[rootCauseHypothesis]
The Phase 58 D-09 embedded-sequence gate (lines 279-285) allows embedded ∈ {0, 1} to tolerate clients using either pre-increment or post-increment conventions. However, the API then unconditionally creates new entries with sequenceNumber='1' (line 362), not the embedded sequence value. The API does not store the embedded Sequence; it stores its own DB counter. The client's sdk-core binding check (line 275) expects these to match exactly, but on first publish they diverge: embedded=0 (from client pre-increment) vs persisted=1 (API's fixed value).
===== agent-a5694aff9d768b3d0.jsonl =====
area: 

[summary]
The bind_verified function in crates/fuse/src/verify.rs (line 100-104) performs a strict equality comparison between two IPNS sequence counters: the sequence number embedded in the signed CBOR data (decoded from the data field) and the sequence_number from the IpnsResolveResponse returned by the API. On first publish, this binding check rejects legitimate records where embedded=0 but the API's resolve response returns seq=1, treating this as a cryptographic binding failure rather than allowing first-publish records.

[exactComparison]
The comparison on line 100 of crates/fuse/src/verify.rs is:
`if embedded_seq != resp_seq {`
where:
- `embedded_seq` = u64 decoded from the CBOR "Sequence" field (from decode_ipns_cbor_data in crates/core/src/ipns.rs line 119)
- `resp_seq` = the `sequence_number` field from IpnsResolveResponse, parsed as u64 on line 96-99 (IpnsResolveResponse.sequence_number is a String field per crates/api-client/src/types.rs line 136)

[firstPublishBehavior]
On FIRST PUBLISH (is_first_publish=true, no prior resolve): 
1. Embedded Sequence in signed CBOR data: 0 (hardcoded via next_file_publish_sequence returning Ok(0))
2. Persisted/returned in API response: Unknown to this subsystem — publish_ipns returns PublishResult::Success with no sequence field, and the sequence value is later obtained via a separate resolve_ipns call.

Evidence: crates/fuse/src/content_ops.rs lines 115-126 show is_first_publish sets current_seq=None and next_file_publish_sequence(true, None) returns Ok(0). The published IPNS record is created with sequence=0 (line 129), but when resolve_ipns_verified is later called (possibly in replay or by coordinator.resolve_sequence), the API's IpnsResolveResponse may return sequence_number="1", causing the binding mismatch error on line 100-104 of verify.rs.

[rootCauseHypothesis]
The backend API or IPNS implementation may be auto-incrementing or normalizing the sequence number to 1 on first publish (instead of persisting sequence=0), creating a mismatch between what was embedded in the signed CBOR (0) and what the API returns in the resolve response (1). Alternatively, the API's persistence layer initializes IPNS records at sequence=1 rather than sequence=0, treating 0 as "not yet published" or "initial state." The verify.rs binding check does not account for this first-publish boundary condition and treats any mismatch as a cryptographic failure rather than allowing the documented first-publish behavior where sequence should be 0 on creation but may be stored/resolved differently by the backend.
```

> AGENT

The 4th investigator adds the clincher: the **TS SDK file-create path embeds `1n`** (`packages/sdk-core/src/file/index.ts:144`), while the **Rust FUSE path embeds `0`**. So the inconsistency is precisely *Rust-FUSE-first-publish (0) vs TS-SDK/API-contract (1)* — and the API's D-09 `{0,1}` gate was deliberately written to tolerate both. **My resolve-side binding encoded the wrong invariant** (`embedded == DB`) instead of the locked one (`embedded ∈ {0,1}` on first publish, DB=1).

**Decision — Option B (minimal, correct, consistent with locked D-09):** relax the resolve-side sequence binding in both languages to accept the documented first-publish skew (`resp==1 && embedded==0`), keeping strict equality everywhere else and the CID binding fully strict. Unifying the publish paths on `embed=1` is the cleaner long-term cleanup but is a behavioral change to shipped Rust publish code — I'll capture that (plus a TEE-republish-rollback question it raises) as a follow-up todo rather than ride it on a regression hotfix.

```
1	/**
2	 * IPNS Verify Vector Generator
3	 *
4	 * Generates tests/vectors/ipns/verify.json — the shared cross-language
5	 * fixture consumed by both the Rust (crates/fuse/tests/ipns_verify_vectors.rs)
6	 * and the sdk-core (packages/sdk-core/src/__tests__/ipns.test.ts) test suites.
7	 *
8	 * 7 cases (D-11):
9	 *   valid, tampered-sig, name-mismatch, cid-swapped, seq-mismatch,
10	 *   partial-fields, legacy-absent
11	 *
12	 * Run from the repo root (packages/core must be built first):
13	 *   npx tsx scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts
14	 *
15	 * Or run with packages/core as the working directory:
16	 *   cd packages/core && npx tsx ../../scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts
17	 *
18	 * The cid-swapped and seq-mismatch vectors carry REAL Ed25519 signatures over
19	 * their (mis-matching) CBOR data — meaning Ed25519 verification PASSES
20	 * but the binding check (embedded value/seq vs response cid/sequenceNumber)
21	 * fails. This is intentional: these vectors test the binding layer, not
22	 * the signature layer.
23	 */

67	// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any

102	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
103	// Helpers
104	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
105	
106	function hexToBytes(hex: string): Uint8Array {
107	  const bytes = new Uint8Array(hex.length / 2);
108	  for (let i = 0; i < hex.length; i += 2) {
109	    bytes[i / 2] = parseInt(hex.slice(i, i + 2), 16);
110	  }
111	  return bytes;
112	}
113	
114	function bytesToHex(bytes: Uint8Array): string {
115	  return Array.from(bytes)
116	    .map((b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0'))
117	    .join('');
118	}
119	
120	function bytesToBase64(bytes: Uint8Array): string {
121	  return Buffer.from(bytes).toString('base64');
122	}
123	
124	/**
125	 * Build CBOR data matching the Rust build_cbor_data / cipherbox-core layout.
126	 *
127	 * Field order: TTL (int), Value (bytes), Sequence (int), Validity (bytes), ValidityType (int).
128	 * This layout matches what the Rust build_cbor_data encodes and what the ipns npm package
129	 * generates — the same bytes are on both sides of the cross-language boundary.
130	 *
131	 * IMPORTANT: Uses cborg directly so we can build CBOR for "wrong" cid/seq values
132	 * (cid-swapped and seq-mismatch cases) without going through createIpnsRecord.
133	 */
134	function buildCborData(cid: string, sequenceNumber: number): Uint8Array {
135	  return cborEncode({
136	    TTL: 300000000000,
137	    Value: new TextEncoder().encode(`/ipfs/${cid}`),
138	    Sequence: sequenceNumber,
139	    Validity: new TextEncoder().encode('2099-01-01T00:00:00.000000000Z'),
140	    ValidityType: 0,
141	  }) as Uint8Array;
142	}
143	
144	/**
145	 * Build the signed bytes per IPFS IPNS spec:
146	 * "ipns-signature:" || CBOR data
147	 */
148	function buildSignedBytes(cborData: Uint8Array): Uint8Array {
149	  const prefix = new TextEncoder().encode('ipns-signature:');
150	  const signed = new Uint8Array(prefix.length + cborData.length);
151	  signed.set(prefix, 0);
152	  signed.set(cborData, prefix.length);
153	  return signed;
154	}
155	
156	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

160	async function main(): Promise<void> {
161	  const primaryPriv = hexToBytes(PRIMARY_PRIV_KEY_HEX);
162	  const primaryPub = (await ed.getPublicKeyAsync(primaryPriv)) as Uint8Array;
163	  const primaryIpnsName = (await deriveIpnsName(primaryPub)) as string;
164	
165	  const secondaryPriv = hexToBytes(SECONDARY_PRIV_KEY_HEX);
166	  const secondaryPub = (await ed.getPublicKeyAsync(secondaryPriv)) as Uint8Array;
167	  const secondaryIpnsName = (await deriveIpnsName(secondaryPub)) as string;
168	
169	  console.log('Primary IPNS name:', primaryIpnsName);
170	  console.log('Secondary IPNS name:', secondaryIpnsName);
171	  if (primaryIpnsName === secondaryIpnsName) {
172	    throw new Error('Primary and secondary IPNS names must differ');
173	  }
174	
175	  const SEQ = 5;
176	  const SEQ_DIFFERENT = 99;
177

179	
180	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
181	  // Case 1: valid — signature, name, cid, and sequence all match
182	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
183	  {
184	    const cborData = buildCborData(CID_A, SEQ);
185	    const signedBytes = buildSignedBytes(cborData);
186	    const sig = (await ed.signAsync(signedBytes, primaryPriv)) as Uint8Array;
187	
188	    vectors.push({
189	      description: 'valid — signature, name, cid, and sequence all match',
190	      ipns_name: primaryIpnsName,
191	      public_key: bytesToHex(primaryPub),
192	      private_key: PRIMARY_PRIV_KEY_HEX,
193	      cid: CID_A,
194	      sequence_number: String(SEQ),
195	      signature_v2: bytesToBase64(sig),
196	      data: bytesToBase64(cborData),
197	      pub_key: bytesToBase64(primaryPub),
198	      expected_result: 'valid',
199	    });
200	    console.log('Case 1 (valid): done');
201	  }
202	
203	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
204	  // Case 2: tampered-sig — flip one byte of signatureV2 over an
205	  // otherwise-valid record. Ed25519 verification fails → "invalid".
206	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
207	  {
208	    const cborData = buildCborData(CID_A, SEQ);
209	    const signedBytes = buildSignedBytes(cborData);
210	    const sig = (await ed.signAsync(signedBytes, primaryPriv)) as Uint8Array;
211	    const tamperedSig = new Uint8Array(sig);
212	    tamperedSig[0] ^= 0xff;
213	
214	    vectors.push({
215	      description: 'tampered-sig — flip one byte of signatureV2',
216	      ipns_name: primaryIpnsName,
217	      public_key: bytesToHex(primaryPub),
218	      private_key: PRIMARY_PRIV_KEY_HEX,
219	      cid: CID_A,
220	      sequence_number: String(SEQ),
221	      signature_v2: bytesToBase64(tamperedSig),
222	      data: bytesToBase64(cborData),
223	      pub_key: bytesToBase64(primaryPub),
224	      expected_result: 'invalid',
225	    });
226	    console.log('Case 2 (tampered-sig): done');
227	  }
228	
229	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
230	  // Case 3: name-mismatch — CBOR data and sig are from the secondary
231	  // keypair, but ipns_name is the primary name. secondaryPub derives to
232	  // secondaryIpnsName, which does NOT match ipns_name (primaryIpnsName).
233	  // Ed25519 sig is valid; name binding check fails → "invalid".
234	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
235	  {
236	    const cborData = buildCborData(CID_A, SEQ);
237	    const signedBytes = buildSignedBytes(cborData);
238	    const sig = (await ed.signAsync(signedBytes, secondaryPriv)) as Uint8Array;
239	
240	    vectors.push({
241	      description: 'name-mismatch — valid sig but pubKey derives to different IPNS name',
242	      ipns_name: primaryIpnsName,
243	      public_key: bytesToHex(secondaryPub),
244	      private_key: SECONDARY_PRIV_KEY_HEX,
245	      cid: CID_A,
246	      sequence_number: String(SEQ),
247	      signature_v2: bytesToBase64(sig),
248	      data: bytesToBase64(cborData),
249	      pub_key: bytesToBase64(secondaryPub),
250	      expected_result: 'invalid',
251	    });
252	    console.log('Case 3 (name-mismatch): done');
253	  }
254	
255	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
256	  // Case 4: cid-swapped — sig valid over CBOR data containing CID_A,
257	  // but response `cid` field is CID_B.
258	  //
259	  // Ed25519 signature covers: "ipns-signature:" + CBOR{Value="/ipfs/CID_A", ...}
260	  // → verify_ipns_resolve_signature returns Ok(Some(true)).
261	  // Binding check: embedded value "/ipfs/CID_A" != "/ipfs/CID_B" → fail.
262	  // Expected result: "invalid" (caught by binding layer, not sig layer).
263	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
264	  {
265	    const cborData = buildCborData(CID_A, SEQ);
266	    const signedBytes = buildSignedBytes(cborData);
267	    const sig = (await ed.signAsync(signedBytes, primaryPriv)) as Uint8Array;
268	
269	    vectors.push({
270	      description:
271	        'cid-swapped — valid sig over CBOR data with CID_A, but response cid field is CID_B',
272	      ipns_name: primaryIpnsName,
273	      public_key: bytesToHex(primaryPub),
274	      private_key: PRIMARY_PRIV_KEY_HEX,
275	      cid: CID_B,
276	      sequence_number: String(SEQ),
277	      signature_v2: bytesToBase64(sig),
278	      data: bytesToBase64(cborData),
279	      pub_key: bytesToBase64(primaryPub),
280	      expected_result: 'invalid',
281	    });
282	    console.log('Case 4 (cid-swapped): done — sig covers CBOR with CID_A, response.cid=CID_B');
283	  }
284	
285	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
286	  // Case 5: seq-mismatch — sig valid over CBOR data with seq=99,
287	  // but response `sequence_number` is 5.
288	  //
289	  // Ed25519 signature covers: "ipns-signature:" + CBOR{Sequence=99, ...}
290	  // → verify_ipns_resolve_signature returns Ok(Some(true)).
291	  // Binding check: embedded seq 99 != response seq 5 → fail.
292	  // Expected result: "invalid" (caught by binding layer, not sig layer).
293	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
294	  {
295	    const cborData = buildCborData(CID_A, SEQ_DIFFERENT);
296	    const signedBytes = buildSignedBytes(cborData);
297	    const sig = (await ed.signAsync(signedBytes, primaryPriv)) as Uint8Array;
298	
299	    vectors.push({
300	      description:
301	        'seq-mismatch — valid sig over CBOR data with seq=99, but response sequenceNumber is 5',
302	      ipns_name: primaryIpnsName,
303	      public_key: bytesToHex(primaryPub),
304	      private_key: PRIMARY_PRIV_KEY_HEX,
305	      cid: CID_A,
306	      sequence_number: String(SEQ),
307	      signature_v2: bytesToBase64(sig),
308	      data: bytesToBase64(cborData),
309	      pub_key: bytesToBase64(primaryPub),
310	      expected_result: 'invalid',
311	    });
312	    console.log('Case 5 (seq-mismatch): done — sig covers CBOR with seq=99, response.seq=5');
313	  }
314	
315	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
316	  // Case 6: partial-fields (downgrade vector) — only signatureV2 present,
317	  // data and pub_key are null. Fails the partial-fields guard before
318	  // Ed25519 verification is even attempted.
319	  // Expected result: "invalid" (fail-closed on partial fields).
320	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
321	  {
322	    const cborData = buildCborData(CID_A, SEQ);
323	    const signedBytes = buildSignedBytes(cborData);
324	    const sig = (await ed.signAsync(signedBytes, primaryPriv)) as Uint8Array;
325	
326	    vectors.push({
327	      description:
328	        'partial-fields — only signatureV2 present, data and pub_key null (downgrade vector)',
329	      ipns_name: primaryIpnsName,
330	      public_key: bytesToHex(primaryPub),
331	      private_key: PRIMARY_PRIV_KEY_HEX,
332	      cid: CID_A,
333	      sequence_number: String(SEQ),
334	      signature_v2: bytesToBase64(sig),
335	      data: null,
336	      pub_key: null,
337	      expected_result: 'invalid',
338	    });
339	    console.log('Case 6 (partial-fields): done');
340	  }
341	
342	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
343	  // Case 7: legacy-absent — all three of signatureV2, data, pub_key null.
344	  // Only all-absent records are treated as legacy (D-04).
345	  // Expected result: "legacy" (allowed, signatureVerified=false).
346	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
347	  {
348	    vectors.push({
349	      description: 'legacy-absent — all three signature fields null (pre-signing legacy record)',
350	      ipns_name: primaryIpnsName,
351	      public_key: bytesToHex(primaryPub),
352	      private_key: PRIMARY_PRIV_KEY_HEX,
353	      cid: CID_A,
354	      sequence_number: String(SEQ),
355	      signature_v2: null,
356	      data: null,
357	      pub_key: null,
358	      expected_result: 'legacy',
359	    });
360	    console.log('Case 7 (legacy-absent): done');
361	  }
362	
363	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
364	  // Sanity checks
365	  // ------------------------------------------------------------------
366	  if (vectors.length !== 7) {
367	    throw new Error(`Expected 7 vectors, got ${vectors.length}`);
368	  }
369	
370	  const expectedResults = [
371	    'valid',
372	    'invalid',
373	    'invalid',
374	    'invalid',
375	    'invalid',
376	    'invalid',
377	    'legacy',
378	  ];
379	  const expectedDescriptions = [
380	    'valid',
381	    'tampered-sig',
382	    'name-mismatch',
383	    'cid-swapped',
384	    'seq-mismatch',
385	    'partial-fields',
386	    'legacy-absent',
387	  ];
388	  for (let i = 0; i < vectors.length; i++) {
389	    if (vectors[i].expected_result !== expectedResults[i]) {
390	      throw new Error(
391	        `Vector ${i} expected_result mismatch: got ${vectors[i].expected_result}, want ${expectedResults[i]}`
392	      );
393	    }
394	    if (!vectors[i].description.startsWith(expectedDescriptions[i])) {
395	      throw new Error(
396	        `Vector ${i} description should start with "${expectedDescriptions[i]}", got "${vectors[i].description}"`
397	      );
398	    }
399	  }
400	
401	  const outPath = join(REPO_ROOT, 'tests', 'vectors', 'ipns', 'verify.json');
402	  writeFileSync(outPath, JSON.stringify(vectors, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf-8');
403	  console.log(`\nWrote ${vectors.length} vectors to: ${outPath}`);
404	  console.log('expected_results:', vectors.map((v) => v.expected_result).join(', '));
405	}
406	
407	main().catch((err) => {
408	  console.error('Error:', err);
409	  process.exit(1);
410	});
```

```
1	//! Cross-language IPNS verify test vectors.
2	//!
3	//! Loads the shared fixture from tests/vectors/ipns/verify.json and validates
4	//! each case against the real `verify_ipns_resolve_signature` (from
5	//! `cipherbox-api-client`) and `decode_ipns_cbor_data` (from `cipherbox-core`),
6	//! matching the binding logic in `crates/fuse/src/verify.rs` `bind_verified`.
7	//!
8	//! Located in `crates/fuse` to avoid a dependency cycle: both
9	//! `cipherbox-api-client` and `cipherbox-core` depend on `cipherbox-crypto`, so
10	//! `cipherbox-crypto` cannot dev-depend on either without a cycle. `cipherbox-fuse`
11	//! already depends on both, making it the cycle-free home for this test (D-12).
12	//!
13	//! See: tests/vectors/ipns/verify.json for the 7 cases (D-11).
14	
15	use base64::Engine as _;
16	use base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD;
17	use serde::Deserialize;
18	use std::path::PathBuf;
19	
20	// ============================================================
21	// Vector loading helpers (mirrors cross_language.rs convention)
22	// ============================================================
23	
24	/// Resolve the path to the shared test vectors directory from CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR.
25	/// crates/fuse is two levels below the repo root, so we go ../../tests/vectors.
26	fn vectors_path(subpath: &str) -> PathBuf {
27	    let mut p = PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"));
28	    p.push("../../tests/vectors");
29	    p.push(subpath);
30	    p
31	}
32	
33	/// Load and deserialize a JSON test vector file.
34	fn load_vectors<T: serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(filename: &str) -> Vec<T> {
35	    let path = vectors_path(filename);
36	    let data = std::fs::read_to_string(&path)
37	        .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("Failed to load {}: {}", path.display(), e));
38	    serde_json::from_str(&data).unwrap()
39	}
40	
41	// ============================================================
42	// IPNS Verify Vectors
43	// ============================================================
44	
45	/// Deserializes one entry from tests/vectors/ipns/verify.json.
46	///
47	/// Field names match the fixture schema exactly (snake_case JSON).
48	#[derive(Deserialize)]
49	struct IpnsVerifyVector {
50	    description: String,
51	    ipns_name: String,
52	    cid: String,
53	    sequence_number: String,
54	    signature_v2: Option<String>,
55	    data: Option<String>,
56	    pub_key: Option<String>,
57	    expected_result: String, // "valid" | "invalid" | "legacy"
58	}
59	
60	/// Run the same two-step binding logic as `crates/fuse/src/verify.rs` `bind_verified`:
61	///
62	/// 1. Call `verify_ipns_resolve_signature` → `Option<bool>`.
63	/// 2. For `Some(true)`: base64-decode `data`, call `decode_ipns_cbor_data`, compare
64	///    embedded value to `/ipfs/{resp.cid}` and embedded seq to `resp.sequence_number`.
65	/// 3. Map to "valid" | "invalid" | "legacy".
66	///
67	/// This is equivalent to `bind_verified(&resp, verdict)` but spelled out explicitly so
68	/// the test pins both the api-client signature check and the core CBOR decode path.
69	fn classify_vector(v: &IpnsVerifyVector) -> String {
70	    let resp = cipherbox_api_client::types::IpnsResolveResponse {
71	        success: true,
72	        cid: v.cid.clone(),
73	        sequence_number: v.sequence_number.clone(),
74	        signature_v2: v.signature_v2.clone(),
75	        data: v.data.clone(),
76	        pub_key: v.pub_key.clone(),
77	    };
78	
79	    let verdict =
80	        match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::verify_ipns_resolve_signature(&resp, &v.ipns_name) {
81	            Err(e) => {
82	                eprintln!("[{}] verify error: {}", v.description, e);
83	                return "invalid".to_string();
84	            }
85	            Ok(v) => v,
86	        };
87	
88	    match verdict {
89	        None => "legacy".to_string(),
90	        Some(false) => "invalid".to_string(),
91	        Some(true) => {
92	            // Signature is valid. Perform the CBOR binding check (D-07/D-08).
93	            // This is the same path as bind_verified in fuse/src/verify.rs.
94	            let data_b64 = match resp.data.as_deref() {
95	                Some(s) => s,
96	                None => return "invalid".to_string(),
97	            };
98	            let data_bytes = match STANDARD.decode(data_b64) {
99	                Ok(b) => b,
100	                Err(e) => {
101	                    eprintln!("[{}] base64 decode failed: {}", v.description, e);
102	                    return "invalid".to_string();
103	                }
104	            };
105	
106	            let (embedded_value, embedded_seq) =
107	                match cipherbox_core::ipns::decode_ipns_cbor_data(&data_bytes) {
108	                    Ok(r) => r,
109	                    Err(e) => {
110	                        eprintln!("[{}] CBOR decode failed: {}", v.description, e);
111	                        return "invalid".to_string();
112	                    }
113	                };
114	
115	            // D-08: embedded CBOR value must be "/ipfs/<response.cid>"
116	            let expected_value = format!("/ipfs/{}", resp.cid);
117	            if embedded_value != expected_value {
118	                eprintln!(
119	                    "[{}] cid binding mismatch: embedded={}, response={}",
120	                    v.description, embedded_value, resp.cid
121	                );
122	                return "invalid".to_string();
123	            }
124	
125	            // D-07: embedded seq must match response sequence_number
126	            let resp_seq = match resp.sequence_number.parse::<u64>() {
127	                Ok(s) => s,
128	                Err(e) => {
129	                    eprintln!("[{}] parse sequence_number failed: {}", v.description, e);
130	                    return "invalid".to_string();
131	                }
132	            };
133	            if embedded_seq != resp_seq {
134	                eprintln!(
135	                    "[{}] seq binding mismatch: embedded={}, response={}",
136	                    v.description, embedded_seq, resp_seq
137	                );
138	                return "invalid".to_string();
139	            }
140	
141	            "valid".to_string()
142	        }
143	    }
144	}
145	
146	/// Cross-language IPNS verify parity test.
147	///
148	/// Loads the 7-case shared fixture and asserts that Rust produces the same
149	/// verdict as the expected_result field. This pins:
150	///
151	/// - `cipherbox_api_client::ipns::verify_ipns_resolve_signature` (the signed-bytes
152	///   construction: "ipns-signature:" prefix || CBOR data)
153	/// - `cipherbox_core::ipns::decode_ipns_cbor_data` (the CBOR field extraction)
154	///
155	/// Both are exercised against vectors whose bytes were produced by the JS
156	/// generator (`scripts/gen-ipns-verify-vectors.ts`), so any Rust↔JS drift
157	/// in byte-construction fails this test — satisfying D-12.
158	#[test]
159	fn ipns_verify_cross_language() {
160	    let vectors: Vec<IpnsVerifyVector> = load_vectors("ipns/verify.json");
161	    assert!(!vectors.is_empty(), "No IPNS verify vectors loaded");
162	    assert_eq!(vectors.len(), 7, "Expected exactly 7 IPNS verify vectors");
163	
164	    for v in &vectors {
165	        let actual = classify_vector(v);
166	        assert_eq!(
167	            actual, v.expected_result,
168	            "IPNS verify vector mismatch for: {}",
169	            v.description
170	        );
171	    }
172	}
173
```

> AGENT

The decisive detail: `publish.rs:99` shows `resolve_sequence` returns `verified.sequence_number`, and `bind_verified` currently sets that to **`embedded_seq`** (`verify.rs:115`). For a first-publish file that's `0` → next publish computes `0+1=1`, which the API treats as *idempotent* (==DB 1), not forward — a reverse off-by-one. Fix: set `VerifiedResolve.sequence_number = resp_seq` (the DB counter). For every non-skew record `embedded_seq == resp_seq` so it's a no-op; only the benign first-publish skew differs, and there the DB counter is the correct value for forward math. (The JS side already returns `response.sequenceNumber`, so it needs only the binding relaxation.)

Let me read the rest of the verify.rs unit tests to keep them consistent.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/crates/fuse/src/verify.rs",
  "old_string": "            // D-07: embedded sequence must match response sequence_number.\n            let resp_seq = resp\n                .sequence_number\n                .parse::<u64>()\n                .map_err(|e| VerifyError::Invalid(format!(\"parse response sequence_number: {}\", e)))?;\n            if embedded_seq != resp_seq {\n                return Err(VerifyError::Invalid(format!(\n                    \"IPNS sequence binding mismatch: embedded={}, response seq={}\",\n                    embedded_seq, resp_seq\n                )));\n            }\n\n            // D-08: use the signed/embedded cid (strip \"/ipfs/\" prefix).\n            let cid = embedded_value\n                .strip_prefix(\"/ipfs/\")\n                .unwrap_or(&embedded_value)\n                .to_string();\n\n            Ok(VerifiedResolve {\n                cid,\n                sequence_number: embedded_seq,\n                signature_verified: true,\n            })",
  "new_string": "            // D-07: embedded sequence must match response sequence_number.\n            //\n            // First-publish exception (mirrors the API's publish-side D-09 gate in\n            // ipns.service.ts::upsertFolderIpns): on a brand-new IPNS name the API accepts an\n            // embedded sequence of 0 OR 1 and unconditionally stores DB sequenceNumber=1. The\n            // Rust/FUSE publish paths embed the IPNS-native 0 (next_file_publish_sequence, the\n            // first child-folder publish) while the TS SDK embeds 1 — both legitimate per D-09.\n            // So a resolved first-generation record (resp_seq == 1) may legitimately carry an\n            // embedded 0. Accept that single documented skew; require strict equality for every\n            // other sequence. The cid binding above stays strict and DB CID remains the\n            // authoritative trust root, so this does not widen the attack surface: a record can\n            // only reach the network with embedded 0 while DB is 1 (the first-publish window).\n            let resp_seq = resp\n                .sequence_number\n                .parse::<u64>()\n                .map_err(|e| VerifyError::Invalid(format!(\"parse response sequence_number: {}\", e)))?;\n            let seq_ok = embedded_seq == resp_seq || (resp_seq == 1 && embedded_seq == 0);\n            if !seq_ok {\n                return Err(VerifyError::Invalid(format!(\n                    \"IPNS sequence binding mismatch: embedded={}, response seq={}\",\n                    embedded_seq, resp_seq\n                )));\n            }\n\n            // D-08: use the signed/embedded cid (strip \"/ipfs/\" prefix).\n            let cid = embedded_value\n                .strip_prefix(\"/ipfs/\")\n                .unwrap_or(&embedded_value)\n                .to_string();\n\n            // Return the DB-authoritative sequence (resp_seq): downstream sequence math\n            // (resolve_sequence → next publish = seq + 1) keys off the API's DB counter, and\n            // the binding above guarantees resp_seq == embedded_seq except for the benign\n            // first-publish skew, where resp_seq (1) is the correct forward base.\n            Ok(VerifiedResolve {\n                cid,\n                sequence_number: resp_seq,\n                signature_verified: true,\n            })"
}
```

```json
  "new_string": "        const embeddedSeqBigInt =\n          typeof embeddedSeq === 'bigint' ? embeddedSeq : BigInt(embeddedSeq as number);\n        const responseSeqBigInt = BigInt(response.sequenceNumber);\n        // First-publish exception (mirrors the API's publish-side D-09 gate in\n        // ipns.service.ts::upsertFolderIpns): on a brand-new IPNS name the API accepts an\n        // embedded sequence of 0 OR 1 and unconditionally stores DB sequenceNumber=1. The\n        // Rust/FUSE publish paths embed the IPNS-native 0 while the TS SDK embeds 1 — both\n        // legitimate per D-09. So a resolved first-generation record (sequenceNumber==1) may\n        // legitimately carry an embedded 0. Accept that single documented skew; require strict\n        // equality otherwise. The cid binding above stays strict and DB CID remains the\n        // authoritative trust root, so this does not widen the attack surface (embedded 0 can\n        // only reach the network while DB==1, the first-publish window).\n        const seqOk =\n          embeddedSeqBigInt === responseSeqBigInt ||\n          (responseSeqBigInt === 1n && embeddedSeqBigInt === 0n);\n        if (!seqOk) {\n          throw new Error(\n            `IPNS sequence binding mismatch: embedded=${embeddedSeq}, response sequenceNumber=${response.sequenceNumber}`\n          );\n        }"
}
```

```json
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-22T00:00:00.000Z\ntitle: Unify first-publish IPNS embedded-sequence convention (FUSE 0 vs SDK 1) and verify TEE re-sign path\narea: refactor\nseverity: low\nsource: Phase 58 PR #544 desktop-E2E regression investigation (resolve-binding hotfix f759c4a90)\nfiles:\n  - crates/fuse/src/publish.rs\n  - crates/fuse/src/content_ops.rs\n  - crates/fuse/src/replay.rs\n  - packages/sdk-core/src/file/index.ts\n  - apps/api/src/ipns/ipns.service.ts\n---\n\n## Background\n\nThe Phase 58 resolve-side sequence binding (D-07) was hotfixed (f759c4a90) to accept the\ndocumented first-publish skew: a resolved first-generation record may carry embedded\nSequence=0 while the API returns DB sequenceNumber=1. Root cause is a **client publish-path\ninconsistency**, not a resolve bug:\n\n- **Rust/FUSE** first publish embeds the IPNS-native `0`\n  (`next_file_publish_sequence(true, None) == 0` in `publish.rs`; first child-folder publish\n  in `replay.rs`).\n- **TS SDK** first publish embeds `1n`\n  (`packages/sdk-core/src/file/index.ts` — \"sequence number 1 for new records\").\n- The API (`ipns.service.ts::upsertFolderIpns`) accepts embedded ∈ {0,1} on first publish\n  (D-09 / T-58-08 wedge-poison guard) and **unconditionally stores DB `sequenceNumber: '1'`**.\n\nSo FUSE-published records sit at embedded=0 / DB=1 for their first generation; SDK-published\nrecords sit at embedded=1 / DB=1. Both realign on the first forward update (embedded becomes\nDB+1 thereafter). The hotfix tolerates the skew on resolve and is correct and minimal.\n\n## Action (future hardening phase)\n\n1. **Unify the convention.** Decide whether first publish should embed 0 (IPNS-native) or 1\n   (current API/DB + SDK convention) and make FUSE + SDK consistent. The API comment\n   (`ipns.service.ts:357`) already assumes clients embed `0+1=1`; making FUSE embed 1 would\n   let the resolve binding return to strict `embedded == DB` equality (drop the skew\n   allowance in `verify.rs` + `sdk-core/ipns/index.ts` + the case-8 vector). Touches\n   `next_file_publish_sequence`, the first child-folder publish, and their unit tests\n   (`replay.rs` asserts `NotFound -> new_seq=0`).\n\n2. **Verify the TEE re-sign path is NOT broken by the embed-0 record.** A FUSE-first-published\n   record stores a signedRecord embedding Sequence=0 with DB=1. If the TEE 6-hour republish\n   re-submits that stored record through the D-09 gate, `embeddedSeq(0) < dbSeq(1)` would hit\n   the **\"Rollback rejected\"** branch (`ipns.service.ts:294`). Confirm whether the republish\n   path actually re-runs that gate (it may write directly to the relay without the\n   embedded-sequence check). If it does, FUSE-first-published records cannot be TEE-republished\n   until their first update — a real durability gap. Pre-existing (independent of the resolve\n   hotfix); out of scope for the #544 regression fix.\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Pushed (`fc0c5a754`). Status:

**Regression fixed and pushed.** Root cause: the Phase 58 resolve-side sequence binding used strict `embedded == DB`, stricter than the publish-side D-09 gate it mirrors. First-publish records embed IPNS-native `0` (Rust/FUSE) while the API stores DB `sequenceNumber=1` → `embedded=0, response seq=1` rejected on all platforms. Folder SDK E2E passed because the TS SDK embeds `1`.

**Fix (`f759c4a90`):** relaxed both bindings (Rust `verify.rs` + sdk-core `resolveIpnsRecord`) to accept the one documented skew (`resp==1 && embedded==0`); strict everywhere else, CID binding untouched. `bind_verified` now returns the DB-authoritative seq so forward math stays correct. Added case-8 cross-language vector + Rust/JS unit tests incl. negative scoping (`embedded=0, resp≥2` still rejected).

**Local gates green:** cargo verify 7/7, cross-language vectors 1/1 (8 cases), core ipns 21/21, sdk-core ipns 30/30.

**Cargo.lock:** the `0.7.0` release-preview increment was already committed and is confirmed on the remote; my fix adds no deps so the lock is in sync. All up.

**Follow-up todo (`fc0c5a754`):** captured the publish-path inconsistency (FUSE embeds 0 vs SDK embeds 1) and the open TEE-re-sign-vs-D-09-rollback question for a future hardening phase.

**Now validating:** re-dispatched CI E2E on `f759c4a90` (run [27973760696](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/27973760696)), polling in the background — I'll report once the desktop suites settle and confirm the `binding mismatch` is gone. The merge decision stays with you.

> AGENT

Root cause found — and it's a real, recurring bug. The pin **succeeds** (CID `Qmf6j47...`), but the step captures the *entire* `appleboy/ssh-action` stdout — including the drone-ssh banner:

```
CID="REDACTED
===============================================
✅ Successfully executed commands to all hosts.
==============================================="
```

So `CID` is **multi-line**, and `echo "CID=${CID}" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"` writes a bare multi-line value — which `$GITHUB_ENV` rejects (`Value cannot be null. (Parameter 'name')`), because multi-line env values require heredoc delimiter syntax. Let me read the workflow to fix the CID extraction.

> TOOL
